# Comprehensive management

<table>
<tr><th>Classification</th><th colspan="2">Performance Indicator CM-1 Organization commitment</th></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="3">Comprehensive Management</td><td>Period of application</td><td>Recent 3 years</td></tr>
<tr><td>Performance criteria</td><td>Demonstrated support from all management / executive levels in the water supplier:<br>· organization’s articulated risk management approach (e.g. clear statements of risk ownership at governance and leadership levels, as well as the level of risks the organization are willing (and able) to take on);<br>· leadership makes informed investment decisions aligned with their risk approach; there is a clear link between investment and risk;<br>· everyone across the organization is focused on risk and seeking improvement, identifying, using, and sharing best practice from all relevant sources;<br>· management proactively supports any required changes in work practices to suit risk management approaches;<br>· management provides the necessary resources for sustainable water safety planning (e.g. improvement plans acknowledged and endorsed by senior management and supported by adequate funding, mid-long term plan for system management).</td></tr>
<tr><td>Performance scoring</td><td>Decided by assessment team member consultation in field-survey<br><table>
<tr><th>Emerging</th><th>Progressin g</th><th>Good</th><th>Very Good</th><th>Excellent</th></tr>
<tr><td>1</td><td>2</td><td>3</td><td>4</td><td>5</td></tr>
</table></td></tr>
<tr><td>Weighting of this Performance Indicator</td><td colspan="2">Proportion of the Comprehensive Management Classification: 15%</td></tr>
<tr><td>Explanation of scoring and evidence</td><td colspan="2">Scoring<br>• Excellent: All performance criteria are fully satisfied with some exceeded<br>• Very good: All performance criteria are fully satisfied<br>• Good: Most of the performance criteria are fully satisfied<br>• Progressing: Most of the performance criteria are partially satisfied and progress is being made over time<br>• Emerging: Some performance criteria are partially satisfied Evidence<br>· WSP documents<br>· Water supplier’s Statements (or similar) on Vision, Mission, Strategic Direction/Forward Planning, Structure, Policies and Procedures etc<br>· Interviews with senior management, executive officers, operators</td></tr>
</table>

<table>
<tr><th>Classification</th><th colspan="2">Performance Indicator CM-2 WSP Team</th></tr>
<tr><td rowspan="3">Comprehensive Management</td><td>Period of application</td><td>Recent 3 years</td></tr>
<tr><td>Performance criteria</td><td>The WSP team:<br>· regularly meets to discuss and review performance against the WSP plans<br>· has all members actively involved in water safety planning implementation<br>· adequately represents the key internal and external stakeholders<br>· has access to required specialised knowledge to complement that of the regular WSP team members</td></tr>
<tr><td>Performance scoring</td><td>Decided by assessment team member consultation in field-survey<br><table>
<tr><th>Emerging</th><th>Progressin g</th><th>Good</th><th>Very Good</th><th>Excellent</th></tr>
<tr><td>1</td><td>2</td><td>3</td><td>4</td><td>5</td></tr>
</table></td></tr>
<tr><td>Weighting of this Performance Indicator</td><td colspan="2">Proportion of the Comprehensive Management Classification: 10%</td></tr>
<tr><td>Explanation of scoring and evidence</td><td colspan="2">Scoring<br>• Excellent: All performance criteria are fully satisfied with some exceeded<br>• Very good: All performance criteria are fully satisfied<br>• Good: Most of the performance criteria are fully satisfied<br>• Progressing: Most of the performance criteria are partially satisfied and progress is being made over time<br>• Emerging: Some performance criteria are partially satisfied Evidence<br>· WSP Team meeting minutes documents<br>· Interviews with WSP team members and other management staff</td></tr>
</table>

<table>
<tr><th>Classification</th><th colspan="2">Performance Indicator CM-3 Stakeholder engagement and customer communications</th></tr>

<tr><td>Performance criteria</td><td>· External stakeholders are consulted to inform risk management decisions (e.g. those managing supply steps outside the water supplier’s area of responsibility, for example in the catchments)<br>· Water quality information is openly shared with customers (off line and online)<br>· Consumer information/feedback is used in planning and risk management.</td></tr>

Examples of Standard Operating Procedures

|  | Examplesa Category |
| --- | --- |
| General tasks | · site security inspection<br>· water sample collection<br>· calibrating equipment and online monitoring systems<br>· dealing with user notifications/complaints<br>· record keeping and reporting |
| Source | · routine monitoring of the integrity of a source water storage dam<br>· routine sediment removal from a source water off-take weir<br>· routine screen cleaning at an in-take channel<br>· selective abstraction protocols for seasonal surface water harvesting<br>· response to critical limit alarm for source water turbidity |
| Water treatment plant | · chemical dosing procedures (e.g. coagulation/flocculation, pH correction)<br>· response to critical limit alarm for low chlorine at the plant outlet<br>· manual filter backwashing<br>· jar testing<br>· flow meter calibration<br>· water treatment plant by-pass valve operation and maintenance |
| Distribution and storage | · operating intermittent supplies<br>· response to critical limit alarm for low chlorine in the distribution network<br>· inspection and maintenance of storage tanks<br>· loss of pressure in the distribution network<br>· sanitary repair of water main breaks<br>· response to a dirty-water incident following mains repair/replacement<br>· cleaning, disinfecting and filling water carting tanks<br>· maintenance inspection of a tap stand |

a These examples are broad headings only and the list is not exhaustive. Some of the examples may be applicable to several categories.
